How this is computed. Every nightly snapshot writes the engine's predictions for each horizon — a predicted dip level (the most likely low), a predicted rally level (the most likely high), and per-user target / stop bands. Once a horizon's trading-day window has elapsed, this panel compares those predictions to what actually happened — using intraday highs and lows (a stop-loss order in Trading 212 fires the moment price touches the level, not at the daily close). "Target first / Stop first / Neither" partitions trades by which level got touched first; the three columns sum to 100% for each verdict row.

The 30-day horizon is shown by default — that's the swing trader's primary frame. The longer horizon (a confirmation frame) is collapsed; click to expand. Verdicts unqualified by user apply to both Aidy and Jesse (they share the watchlist universe); a per-user qualifier appears only when they've diverged. Definitions of each label are in the glossary below.
What do ENTER / WAIT / HOLD / TRIM / SKIP / EXIT mean?
ENTER
Open a new position. Conviction is above the ENTER threshold AND no Layer-3 veto fires on this horizon. The price-levels panel above each card shows the suggested entry zone.
WAIT
Don't enter today, but don't dismiss the ticker either. Either (a) conviction is between the WAIT floor and the ENTER threshold, or (b) a Layer-3 veto (catalyst inside horizon, downtrend regime) is temporarily blocking ENTER. Re-evaluates each night.
SKIP
Don't enter — conviction is below the WAIT floor OR a hard veto (e.g. fair-value extreme, Tier-C illiquidity) fires. Re-evaluates each night but the bar to flip back to actionable is higher than for WAIT.
HOLD
Keep the position. Conviction is above the WAIT floor and the original thesis is intact. Default state for entered positions on a quiet, in-range day.
TRIM
Take partial profits — sell some of the position but not all. Triggered when fair-value premium goes ≥2σ above the engine's fair-value mean OR the regime flips from positive to negative on an entered position. Reduce exposure, don't pre-commit to a full exit.
EXIT
Close the entire position. Triggered when conviction falls below the WAIT floor (thesis fully weakened), the stop level is breached, OR the engine deems the trade structurally broken (regime collapse, fair-value catastrophe).

Swing trade — planned dip → rally Wait for the cheaper entry, sell at the realistic peak — vs the trend-follow path above (buy now, ride the +1σ target)
Derived from the same 50,000 Monte Carlo paths as the Price Levels above, but read for a different question: would patient buying at a dip and planned selling at a rally beat buying at current price? An ACTIONABLE verdict means all four gates pass — dip and rally both meaningful, the dip-then-rally sequence is likely enough to plan around, and the reward per unit risked is acceptable. Otherwise this horizon isn't a swing setup — either trend-follow at current price or stand aside.

Conviction trajectory — is the signal durable or flip-flopping?last 20 nights
What this is Each night the engine writes a snapshot containing that night's conviction score for every (ticker × horizon). This panel plots the last 20 nights' scores as a sparkline. A signal that says ENTER 5 nights in a row is more trustworthy than one that flipped ENTER → WAIT → ENTER → SKIP → ENTER over the same 5 nights — the latter pattern means we're at a regime boundary and shouldn't trust today's read too much. The dashed green line marks the ENTER threshold (0.30) for reference.

Market mood — is the stock trending or choppy? uptrend_noisy · 42% confidence
What this is A statistical model classifies the past ~60 sessions into one of five regimes (uptrend-quiet, uptrend-noisy, downtrend, sideways, crisis). The current regime + its confidence drive (1) the daily drift parameter inside Monte Carlo (Layer 1 of conviction) and (2) the Layer-3 ENTER veto when state is downtrend/crisis with ≥70% confidence.
Current state uptrend_noisy — Trending upward but with elevated daily volatility — the direction is right but the path is choppy. 42% confidence
Days in this regime12 sessionslonger = more durable
Implied annual drift (fed to MC)+18.0% per yearthis number directly shifts P(target)

How wild are the daily moves? 50% realized · 30d forecast 138% (↑ rising)
What this is Realized volatility = the standard deviation of daily returns, scaled to an annual figure (multiply by √252). A 30% annualized vol means the stock's typical year-over-year price swing is about 30% of its own price. GARCH(1,1) is a model that captures how today's vol depends on recent vol (vol tends to cluster — calm follows calm, wild follows wild).
Current realized vol (60d trailing)50.5% annualized≈ 3.2% typical daily move
Forecast end-of-30d137.6% annualized
Forecast end-of-60d158.4% annualized
Confidence band (set by tier)wideTier A tight, B moderate, C wide
What this means for you Vol forecast to rise — choppier days ahead. Stops and targets should be set wider to avoid being shaken out by normal noise. Higher vol also means the dip-entry and rally-sell zones in the price-levels panel will be further from current price — the model expects more movement in both directions, so the actionable levels spread out.
Is the price fair? $1.85 vs FV mean $3.00 (-2.56σ)
What this is Multi-method triangulation of fundamental value: forward P/E vs sector peers, discounted-cash-flow where input quality is sufficient, and recent comparable transactions. The output is a RANGE (not a point estimate) reflecting genuine uncertainty in valuation. The σ figure measures how far today's market price sits above (+) or below (−) the range mean, in units of the range's own standard deviation: ±1σ means "roughly within the normal valuation range"; ±2σ means "meaningfully outside it."
Fair-value range (low — mean — high)$2.10 — $3.00 — $3.90
Current market price$1.85-2.56σ vs FV mean
Methods contributingAnalyst PT consensus
What this means for you Meaningfully cheap — well below the fair-value mean. Supports new entries (no Layer-3 veto from valuation).
Sanity check — do the two forecast methods agree? all 6 comparisons agree — no haircut
What this is We compute the same forecast (P(target hit first), P(stop hit first), expected value) two completely different ways: random simulation (50,000 Monte Carlo paths) AND solving a deterministic differential equation. The methods make different mathematical assumptions — when they agree, the answer is robust; when they diverge, at least one is being stretched by unusual conditions, so we don't know which to trust. Conviction gets a Layer-2 haircut when they disagree by more than 5.0pp.
Headline All 6 comparisons agree within tolerance — both methods independently arrive at the same answer. No Layer-2 haircut applied to conviction.

Is this stock behaving like its label? ✓ watchlist C → measured C (match)
What this is Each ticker has a tier label you set in the watchlist — A mega-cap mature (think NVDA-like), B mid-cap growth (think AMAT-like), C small-cap fat-tailed (think IONQ-like). The pipeline uses your label to pick which statistical assumptions to run. This panel independently measures the stock's actual recent behavior — vol, vol-of-vol, liquidity, history — and tells you whether the label you set still matches reality. If the math has migrated tiers (e.g. a B-anchored name now behaving like C), the conviction confidence gets a Layer-2 haircut.

What this is A statistical model classifies the past ~60 sessions into one of five regimes (uptrend-quiet, uptrend-noisy, downtrend, sideways, crisis). The current regime + its confidence drive (1) the daily drift parameter inside Monte Carlo (Layer 1 of conviction) and (2) the Layer-3 ENTER veto when state is downtrend/crisis with ≥70% confidence.

How wild are the daily moves? 163% realized · 30d forecast 173% (↑ rising)
What this is Realized volatility = the standard deviation of daily returns, scaled to an annual figure (multiply by √252). A 30% annualized vol means the stock's typical year-over-year price swing is about 30% of its own price. GARCH(1,1) is a model that captures how today's vol depends on recent vol (vol tends to cluster — calm follows calm, wild follows wild).
Current realized vol (60d trailing)163.4% annualized≈ 10.3% typical daily move
Forecast end-of-30d173.3% annualized
Forecast end-of-60d178.2% annualized
Confidence band (set by tier)wideTier A tight, B moderate, C wide
What this means for you Vol forecast roughly flat — set stop/target distances proportional to current realized vol. Higher vol also means the dip-entry and rally-sell zones in the price-levels panel will be further from current price — the model expects more movement in both directions, so the actionable levels spread out.
Is the price fair? $13.89 vs FV mean $20.00 (-2.04σ)
What this is Multi-method triangulation of fundamental value: forward P/E vs sector peers, discounted-cash-flow where input quality is sufficient, and recent comparable transactions. The output is a RANGE (not a point estimate) reflecting genuine uncertainty in valuation. The σ figure measures how far today's market price sits above (+) or below (−) the range mean, in units of the range's own standard deviation: ±1σ means "roughly within the normal valuation range"; ±2σ means "meaningfully outside it."
Fair-value range (low — mean — high)$14.00 — $20.00 — $26.00
Current market price$13.89-2.04σ vs FV mean

What this means for you Meaningfully cheap — well below the fair-value mean. Supports new entries (no Layer-3 veto from valuation).
Sanity check — do the two forecast methods agree? all 6 comparisons agree — no haircut
What this is We compute the same forecast (P(target hit first), P(stop hit first), expected value) two completely different ways: random simulation (50,000 Monte Carlo paths) AND solving a deterministic differential equation. The methods make different mathematical assumptions — when they agree, the answer is robust; when they diverge, at least one is being stretched by unusual conditions, so we don't know which to trust. Conviction gets a Layer-2 haircut when they disagree by more than 5.0pp.
Headline All 6 comparisons agree within tolerance — both methods independently arrive at the same answer. No Layer-2 haircut applied to conviction.

Is this stock behaving like its label? ✓ watchlist C → measured C (match)
What this is Each ticker has a tier label you set in the watchlist — A mega-cap mature (think NVDA-like), B mid-cap growth (think AMAT-like), C small-cap fat-tailed (think IONQ-like). The pipeline uses your label to pick which statistical assumptions to run. This panel independently measures the stock's actual recent behavior — vol, vol-of-vol, liquidity, history — and tells you whether the label you set still matches reality. If the math has migrated tiers (e.g. a B-anchored name now behaving like C), the conviction confidence gets a Layer-2 haircut.

The σ figure measures how far today's market price sits above (+) or below (−) the range mean, in units of the range's own standard deviation: ±1σ means "roughly within the normal valuation range"; ±2σ means "meaningfully outside it."
Fair-value range (low — mean — high)$2.07 — $49.50 — $49.50
Current market price$22.62-2.27σ vs FV mean

Sanity check — do the two forecast methods agree? all 6 comparisons agree — no haircut
What this is We compute the same forecast (P(target hit first), P(stop hit first), expected value) two completely different ways: random simulation (50,000 Monte Carlo paths) AND solving a deterministic differential equation. The methods make different mathematical assumptions — when they agree, the answer is robust; when they diverge, at least one is being stretched by unusual conditions, so we don't know which to trust. Conviction gets a Layer-2 haircut when they disagree by more than 5.0pp.
Headline All 6 comparisons agree within tolerance — both methods independently arrive at the same answer. No Layer-2 haircut applied to conviction.

How wild are the daily moves? 117% realized · 30d forecast 122% (↑ rising)
What this is Realized volatility = the standard deviation of daily returns, scaled to an annual figure (multiply by √252). A 30% annualized vol means the stock's typical year-over-year price swing is about 30% of its own price. GARCH(1,1) is a model that captures how today's vol depends on recent vol (vol tends to cluster — calm follows calm, wild follows wild).

How wild are the daily moves? 111% realized · 30d forecast 95% (↓ falling)
What this is Realized volatility = the standard deviation of daily returns, scaled to an annual figure (multiply by √252). A 30% annualized vol means the stock's typical year-over-year price swing is about 30% of its own price. GARCH(1,1) is a model that captures how today's vol depends on recent vol (vol tends to cluster — calm follows calm, wild follows wild).
Current realized vol (60d trailing)110.7% annualized≈ 7.0% typical daily move

Forecast end-of-60d89.5% annualized

Higher vol also means the dip-entry and rally-sell zones in the price-levels panel will be further from current price — the model expects more movement in both directions, so the actionable levels spread out.
Is the price fair? $937.11 vs FV mean $1500.00 (-1.37σ)
What this is Multi-method triangulation of fundamental value: forward P/E vs sector peers, discounted-cash-flow where input quality is sufficient, and recent comparable transactions. The output is a RANGE (not a point estimate) reflecting genuine uncertainty in valuation. The σ figure measures how far today's market price sits above (+) or below (−) the range mean, in units of the range's own standard deviation: ±1σ means "roughly within the normal valuation range"; ±2σ means "meaningfully outside it."
Fair-value range (low — mean — high)$53.10 — $1500.00 — $1691.06
Current market price$937.11-1.37σ vs FV mean
Methods contributingDCF, Forward P/E peers, Analyst PT consensus
What this means for you Modestly cheap — slight tailwind for new entries.
Sanity check — do the two forecast methods agree? all 6 comparisons agree — no haircut
What this is We compute the same forecast (P(target hit first), P(stop hit first), expected value) two completely different ways: random simulation (50,000 Monte Carlo paths) AND solving a deterministic differential equation. The methods make different mathematical assumptions — when they agree, the answer is robust; when they diverge, at least one is being stretched by unusual conditions, so we don't know which to trust. Conviction gets a Layer-2 haircut when they disagree by more than 5.0pp.
Headline All 6 comparisons agree within tolerance — both methods independently arrive at the same answer. No Layer-2 haircut applied to conviction.

Is this stock behaving like its label? ⚠ watchlist B → measured C (stricter)
What this is Each ticker has a tier label you set in the watchlist — A mega-cap mature (think NVDA-like), B mid-cap growth (think AMAT-like), C small-cap fat-tailed (think IONQ-like). The pipeline uses your label to pick which statistical assumptions to run. This panel independently measures the stock's actual recent behavior — vol, vol-of-vol, liquidity, history — and tells you whether the label you set still matches reality. If the math has migrated tiers (e.g. a B-anchored name now behaving like C), the conviction confidence gets a Layer-2 haircut.

Derived from the same 50,000 Monte Carlo paths as the Price Levels above, but read for a different question: would patient buying at a dip and planned selling at a rally beat buying at current price? An ACTIONABLE verdict means all four gates pass — dip and rally both meaningful, the dip-then-rally sequence is likely enough to plan around, and the reward per unit risked is acceptable. Otherwise this horizon isn't a swing setup — either trend-follow at current price or stand aside.

Market mood — is the stock trending or choppy? crisis · 47% confidence
What this is A statistical model classifies the past ~60 sessions into one of five regimes (uptrend-quiet, uptrend-noisy, downtrend, sideways, crisis). The current regime + its confidence drive (1) the daily drift parameter inside Monte Carlo (Layer 1 of conviction) and (2) the Layer-3 ENTER veto when state is downtrend/crisis with ≥70% confidence.
Current state crisis — Extreme volatility, breakdown of normal regime structure — typical of macro shocks or company-specific catastrophes. 47% confidence
Days in this regime26 sessionslonger = more durable
Implied annual drift (fed to MC)+0.0% per yearthis number directly shifts P(target)
ASTS is in a high-volatility breakdown regime (47% confidence). Observed features over last 60 sessions: trend -91.8%/yr implied, realized vol 118%/yr, vol-of-vol 17%. 26 sessions in this regime — persistent. Nearest alternative is downtrend at 30% — the call is at a boundary, treat with care.
What this means for you Drift fed to MC reflects regime direction. Watch for confidence ≥ 70% if state is downtrend/crisis — that threshold triggers the Layer-3 ENTER veto.
How wild are the daily moves? 118% realized · 30d forecast 112% (↓ falling)
What this is Realized volatility = the standard deviation of daily returns, scaled to an annual figure (multiply by √252). A 30% annualized vol means the stock's typical year-over-year price swing is about 30% of its own price. GARCH(1,1) is a model that captures how today's vol depends on recent vol (vol tends to cluster — calm follows calm, wild follows wild).
Current realized vol (60d trailing)117.7% annualized≈ 7.4% typical daily move

Forecast end-of-60d111.5% annualized

What this means for you Vol forecast roughly flat — set stop/target distances proportional to current realized vol. Higher vol also means the dip-entry and rally-sell zones in the price-levels panel will be further from current price — the model expects more movement in both directions, so the actionable levels spread out.
Is the price fair? $66.43 vs FV mean $95.50 (-2.03σ)
What this is Multi-method triangulation of fundamental value: forward P/E vs sector peers, discounted-cash-flow where input quality is sufficient, and recent comparable transactions. The output is a RANGE (not a point estimate) reflecting genuine uncertainty in valuation. The σ figure measures how far today's market price sits above (+) or below (−) the range mean, in units of the range's own standard deviation: ±1σ means "roughly within the normal valuation range"; ±2σ means "meaningfully outside it."
Fair-value range (low — mean — high)$66.85 — $95.50 — $124.15

Sanity check — do the two forecast methods agree? all 6 comparisons agree — no haircut
What this is We compute the same forecast (P(target hit first), P(stop hit first), expected value) two completely different ways: random simulation (50,000 Monte Carlo paths) AND solving a deterministic differential equation. The methods make different mathematical assumptions — when they agree, the answer is robust; when they diverge, at least one is being stretched by unusual conditions, so we don't know which to trust. Conviction gets a Layer-2 haircut when they disagree by more than 5.0pp.
Headline All 6 comparisons agree within tolerance — both methods independently arrive at the same answer. No Layer-2 haircut applied to conviction.

Is this stock behaving like its label? ✓ watchlist C → measured C (match)
What this is Each ticker has a tier label you set in the watchlist — A mega-cap mature (think NVDA-like), B mid-cap growth (think AMAT-like), C small-cap fat-tailed (think IONQ-like). The pipeline uses your label to pick which statistical assumptions to run. This panel independently measures the stock's actual recent behavior — vol, vol-of-vol, liquidity, history — and tells you whether the label you set still matches reality. If the math has migrated tiers (e.g. a B-anchored name now behaving like C), the conviction confidence gets a Layer-2 haircut.

Market mood — is the stock trending or choppy? crisis · 38% confidence
What this is A statistical model classifies the past ~60 sessions into one of five regimes (uptrend-quiet, uptrend-noisy, downtrend, sideways, crisis). The current regime + its confidence drive (1) the daily drift parameter inside Monte Carlo (Layer 1 of conviction) and (2) the Layer-3 ENTER veto when state is downtrend/crisis with ≥70% confidence.
Current state crisis — Extreme volatility, breakdown of normal regime structure — typical of macro shocks or company-specific catastrophes. 38% confidence

How wild are the daily moves? 102% realized · 30d forecast 98% (↓ falling)
What this is Realized volatility = the standard deviation of daily returns, scaled to an annual figure (multiply by √252). A 30% annualized vol means the stock's typical year-over-year price swing is about 30% of its own price. GARCH(1,1) is a model that captures how today's vol depends on recent vol (vol tends to cluster — calm follows calm, wild follows wild).
Current realized vol (60d trailing)102.3% annualized≈ 6.4% typical daily move
Forecast end-of-30d98.2% annualized
Forecast end-of-60d97.0% annualized
Confidence band (set by tier)moderateTier A tight, B moderate, C wide
What this means for you Vol forecast roughly flat — set stop/target distances proportional to current realized vol. Higher vol also means the dip-entry and rally-sell zones in the price-levels panel will be further from current price — the model expects more movement in both directions, so the actionable levels spread out.
Is the price fair? $827.60 vs FV mean $410.14 (+1.61σ)
What this is Multi-method triangulation of fundamental value: forward P/E vs sector peers, discounted-cash-flow where input quality is sufficient, and recent comparable transactions. The output is a RANGE (not a point estimate) reflecting genuine uncertainty in valuation. The σ figure measures how far today's market price sits above (+) or below (−) the range mean, in units of the range's own standard deviation: ±1σ means "roughly within the normal valuation range"; ±2σ means "meaningfully outside it."
Fair-value range (low — mean — high)$32.31 — $410.14 — $1070.00
Current market price$827.60+1.61σ vs FV mean
Methods contributingDCF, Forward P/E peers, Analyst PT consensus
What this means for you Approaching the +2σ veto threshold — close to triggering TRIM for entered positions and SKIP for watching.
Sanity check — do the two forecast methods agree? all 6 comparisons agree — no haircut
What this is We compute the same forecast (P(target hit first), P(stop hit first), expected value) two completely different ways: random simulation (50,000 Monte Carlo paths) AND solving a deterministic differential equation. The methods make different mathematical assumptions — when they agree, the answer is robust; when they diverge, at least one is being stretched by unusual conditions, so we don't know which to trust. Conviction gets a Layer-2 haircut when they disagree by more than 5.0pp.
Headline All 6 comparisons agree within tolerance — both methods independently arrive at the same answer. No Layer-2 haircut applied to conviction.

Is this stock behaving like its label? ⚠ watchlist B → measured C (stricter)
What this is Each ticker has a tier label you set in the watchlist — A mega-cap mature (think NVDA-like), B mid-cap growth (think AMAT-like), C small-cap fat-tailed (think IONQ-like). The pipeline uses your label to pick which statistical assumptions to run. This panel independently measures the stock's actual recent behavior — vol, vol-of-vol, liquidity, history — and tells you whether the label you set still matches reality. If the math has migrated tiers (e.g. a B-anchored name now behaving like C), the conviction confidence gets a Layer-2 haircut.

Swing trade — planned dip → rally Wait for the cheaper entry, sell at the realistic peak — vs the trend-follow path above (buy now, ride the +1σ target)
Derived from the same 50,000 Monte Carlo paths as the Price Levels above, but read for a different question: would patient buying at a dip and planned selling at a rally beat buying at current price? An ACTIONABLE verdict means all four gates pass — dip and rally both meaningful, the dip-then-rally sequence is likely enough to plan around, and the reward per unit risked is acceptable. Otherwise this horizon isn't a swing setup — either trend-follow at current price or stand aside.

Market mood — is the stock trending or choppy? uptrend_noisy · 38% confidence
What this is A statistical model classifies the past ~60 sessions into one of five regimes (uptrend-quiet, uptrend-noisy, downtrend, sideways, crisis). The current regime + its confidence drive (1) the daily drift parameter inside Monte Carlo (Layer 1 of conviction) and (2) the Layer-3 ENTER veto when state is downtrend/crisis with ≥70% confidence.
Current state uptrend_noisy — Trending upward but with elevated daily volatility — the direction is right but the path is choppy. 38% confidence
Days in this regime61 sessionslonger = more durable
Implied annual drift (fed to MC)+18.0% per yearthis number directly shifts P(target)
AMAT is trending up but with elevated daily swings (38% confidence). Observed features over last 60 sessions: trend +11.2%/yr implied, realized vol 87%/yr, vol-of-vol 9%. 61 sessions in this regime — persistent. Nearest alternative is sideways at 20% — the call is at a boundary, treat with care.
What this means for you Regime supports new entries: drift fed to MC is positive, no Layer-3 veto. The conviction breakdown still has to clear the ENTER threshold — regime is necessary but not sufficient.
How wild are the daily moves? 87% realized · 30d forecast 73% (↓ falling)
What this is Realized volatility = the standard deviation of daily returns, scaled to an annual figure (multiply by √252). A 30% annualized vol means the stock's typical year-over-year price swing is about 30% of its own price. GARCH(1,1) is a model that captures how today's vol depends on recent vol (vol tends to cluster — calm follows calm, wild follows wild).
Current realized vol (60d trailing)86.7% annualized≈ 5.5% typical daily move
Forecast end-of-30d73.2% annualized
Forecast end-of-60d67.9% annualized
Confidence band (set by tier)moderateTier A tight, B moderate, C wide
What this means for you Vol forecast to fall meaningfully — calmer days expected. Tighter stops can be justified. Higher vol also means the dip-entry and rally-sell zones in the price-levels panel will be further from current price — the model expects more movement in both directions, so the actionable levels spread out.
Is the price fair? $496.17 vs FV mean $419.84 (+0.57σ)
What this is Multi-method triangulation of fundamental value: forward P/E vs sector peers, discounted-cash-flow where input quality is sufficient, and recent comparable transactions. The output is a RANGE (not a point estimate) reflecting genuine uncertainty in valuation. The σ figure measures how far today's market price sits above (+) or below (−) the range mean, in units of the range's own standard deviation: ±1σ means "roughly within the normal valuation range"; ±2σ means "meaningfully outside it."
Fair-value range (low — mean — high)$114.55 — $419.84 — $650.00
Current market price$496.17+0.57σ vs FV mean
Methods contributingDCF, Forward P/E peers, Analyst PT consensus
What this means for you Modestly expensive — headwind for new entries but no veto. Entered positions remain HOLD.
Sanity check — do the two forecast methods agree? all 6 comparisons agree — no haircut
What this is We compute the same forecast (P(target hit first), P(stop hit first), expected value) two completely different ways: random simulation (50,000 Monte Carlo paths) AND solving a deterministic differential equation. The methods make different mathematical assumptions — when they agree, the answer is robust; when they diverge, at least one is being stretched by unusual conditions, so we don't know which to trust. Conviction gets a Layer-2 haircut when they disagree by more than 5.0pp.
Headline All 6 comparisons agree within tolerance — both methods independently arrive at the same answer. No Layer-2 haircut applied to conviction.

Is this stock behaving like its label? ⚠ watchlist B → measured C (stricter)
What this is Each ticker has a tier label you set in the watchlist — A mega-cap mature (think NVDA-like), B mid-cap growth (think AMAT-like), C small-cap fat-tailed (think IONQ-like). The pipeline uses your label to pick which statistical assumptions to run. This panel independently measures the stock's actual recent behavior — vol, vol-of-vol, liquidity, history — and tells you whether the label you set still matches reality. If the math has migrated tiers (e.g. a B-anchored name now behaving like C), the conviction confidence gets a Layer-2 haircut.

How wild are the daily moves? 96% realized · 30d forecast 95% (→ flat)
What this is Realized volatility = the standard deviation of daily returns, scaled to an annual figure (multiply by √252). A 30% annualized vol means the stock's typical year-over-year price swing is about 30% of its own price. GARCH(1,1) is a model that captures how today's vol depends on recent vol (vol tends to cluster — calm follows calm, wild follows wild).

Is the price fair? $75.84 vs FV mean $120.00 (-2.45σ)
What this is Multi-method triangulation of fundamental value: forward P/E vs sector peers, discounted-cash-flow where input quality is sufficient, and recent comparable transactions. The output is a RANGE (not a point estimate) reflecting genuine uncertainty in valuation. The σ figure measures how far today's market price sits above (+) or below (−) the range mean, in units of the range's own standard deviation: ±1σ means "roughly within the normal valuation range"; ±2σ means "meaningfully outside it."

Sanity check — do the two forecast methods agree? all 6 comparisons agree — no haircut
What this is We compute the same forecast (P(target hit first), P(stop hit first), expected value) two completely different ways: random simulation (50,000 Monte Carlo paths) AND solving a deterministic differential equation. The methods make different mathematical assumptions — when they agree, the answer is robust; when they diverge, at least one is being stretched by unusual conditions, so we don't know which to trust. Conviction gets a Layer-2 haircut when they disagree by more than 5.0pp.

Market mood — is the stock trending or choppy? uptrend_noisy · 38% confidence
What this is A statistical model classifies the past ~60 sessions into one of five regimes (uptrend-quiet, uptrend-noisy, downtrend, sideways, crisis). The current regime + its confidence drive (1) the daily drift parameter inside Monte Carlo (Layer 1 of conviction) and (2) the Layer-3 ENTER veto when state is downtrend/crisis with ≥70% confidence.
Current state uptrend_noisy — Trending upward but with elevated daily volatility — the direction is right but the path is choppy. 38% confidence
Days in this regime61 sessionslonger = more durable
Implied annual drift (fed to MC)+18.0% per yearthis number directly shifts P(target)
GHM is trending up but with elevated daily swings (38% confidence). Observed features over last 60 sessions: trend +8.7%/yr implied, realized vol 66%/yr, vol-of-vol 7%. 61 sessions in this regime — persistent. Nearest alternative is sideways at 23% — the call is at a boundary, treat with care.
What this means for you Regime supports new entries: drift fed to MC is positive, no Layer-3 veto. The conviction breakdown still has to clear the ENTER threshold — regime is necessary but not sufficient.
How wild are the daily moves? 66% realized · 30d forecast 61% (↓ falling)
What this is Realized volatility = the standard deviation of daily returns, scaled to an annual figure (multiply by √252). A 30% annualized vol means the stock's typical year-over-year price swing is about 30% of its own price. GARCH(1,1) is a model that captures how today's vol depends on recent vol (vol tends to cluster — calm follows calm, wild follows wild).
Current realized vol (60d trailing)66.4% annualized≈ 4.2% typical daily move
Forecast end-of-30d61.2% annualized
Forecast end-of-60d58.4% annualized
Confidence band (set by tier)wideTier A tight, B moderate, C wide
What this means for you Vol forecast roughly flat — set stop/target distances proportional to current realized vol. Higher vol also means the dip-entry and rally-sell zones in the price-levels panel will be further from current price — the model expects more movement in both directions, so the actionable levels spread out.
Is the price fair? $104.81 vs FV mean $132.50 (-1.01σ)
What this is Multi-method triangulation of fundamental value: forward P/E vs sector peers, discounted-cash-flow where input quality is sufficient, and recent comparable transactions. The output is a RANGE (not a point estimate) reflecting genuine uncertainty in valuation. The σ figure measures how far today's market price sits above (+) or below (−) the range mean, in units of the range's own standard deviation: ±1σ means "roughly within the normal valuation range"; ±2σ means "meaningfully outside it."
Fair-value range (low — mean — high)$23.29 — $132.50 — $132.50
Current market price$104.81-1.01σ vs FV mean
Methods contributingForward P/E peers, Analyst PT consensus

Sanity check — do the two forecast methods agree? all 6 comparisons agree — no haircut
What this is We compute the same forecast (P(target hit first), P(stop hit first), expected value) two completely different ways: random simulation (50,000 Monte Carlo paths) AND solving a deterministic differential equation. The methods make different mathematical assumptions — when they agree, the answer is robust; when they diverge, at least one is being stretched by unusual conditions, so we don't know which to trust. Conviction gets a Layer-2 haircut when they disagree by more than 5.0pp.
Headline All 6 comparisons agree within tolerance — both methods independently arrive at the same answer. No Layer-2 haircut applied to conviction.

Is this stock behaving like its label? ✓ watchlist C → measured C (match)
What this is Each ticker has a tier label you set in the watchlist — A mega-cap mature (think NVDA-like), B mid-cap growth (think AMAT-like), C small-cap fat-tailed (think IONQ-like). The pipeline uses your label to pick which statistical assumptions to run. This panel independently measures the stock's actual recent behavior — vol, vol-of-vol, liquidity, history — and tells you whether the label you set still matches reality. If the math has migrated tiers (e.g. a B-anchored name now behaving like C), the conviction confidence gets a Layer-2 haircut.

How wild are the daily moves? 87% realized · 30d forecast 82% (↓ falling)
What this is Realized volatility = the standard deviation of daily returns, scaled to an annual figure (multiply by √252). A 30% annualized vol means the stock's typical year-over-year price swing is about 30% of its own price. GARCH(1,1) is a model that captures how today's vol depends on recent vol (vol tends to cluster — calm follows calm, wild follows wild).
Current realized vol (60d trailing)87.2% annualized≈ 5.5% typical daily move
Forecast end-of-30d81.9% annualized
Forecast end-of-60d78.6% annualized
Confidence band (set by tier)moderateTier A tight, B moderate, C wide
What this means for you Vol forecast roughly flat — set stop/target distances proportional to current realized vol. Higher vol also means the dip-entry and rally-sell zones in the price-levels panel will be further from current price — the model expects more movement in both directions, so the actionable levels spread out.
Is the price fair? $832.56 vs FV mean $856.59 (-0.16σ)
What this is Multi-method triangulation of fundamental value: forward P/E vs sector peers, discounted-cash-flow where input quality is sufficient, and recent comparable transactions. The output is a RANGE (not a point estimate) reflecting genuine uncertainty in valuation. The σ figure measures how far today's market price sits above (+) or below (−) the range mean, in units of the range's own standard deviation: ±1σ means "roughly within the normal valuation range"; ±2σ means "meaningfully outside it."
Fair-value range (low — mean — high)$495.99 — $856.59 — $1090.00
Current market price$832.56-0.16σ vs FV mean
Methods contributingDCF, Forward P/E peers, Analyst PT consensus
What this means for you Fairly valued — no valuation-driven push in either direction.
Sanity check — do the two forecast methods agree? all 6 comparisons agree — no haircut
What this is We compute the same forecast (P(target hit first), P(stop hit first), expected value) two completely different ways: random simulation (50,000 Monte Carlo paths) AND solving a deterministic differential equation. The methods make different mathematical assumptions — when they agree, the answer is robust; when they diverge, at least one is being stretched by unusual conditions, so we don't know which to trust. Conviction gets a Layer-2 haircut when they disagree by more than 5.0pp.
Headline All 6 comparisons agree within tolerance — both methods independently arrive at the same answer. No Layer-2 haircut applied to conviction.

Is this stock behaving like its label? ⚠ watchlist B → measured C (stricter)
What this is Each ticker has a tier label you set in the watchlist — A mega-cap mature (think NVDA-like), B mid-cap growth (think AMAT-like), C small-cap fat-tailed (think IONQ-like). The pipeline uses your label to pick which statistical assumptions to run. This panel independently measures the stock's actual recent behavior — vol, vol-of-vol, liquidity, history — and tells you whether the label you set still matches reality. If the math has migrated tiers (e.g. a B-anchored name now behaving like C), the conviction confidence gets a Layer-2 haircut.

Swing trade — planned dip → rally Wait for the cheaper entry, sell at the realistic peak — vs the trend-follow path above (buy now, ride the +1σ target)
Derived from the same 50,000 Monte Carlo paths as the Price Levels above, but read for a different question: would patient buying at a dip and planned selling at a rally beat buying at current price? An ACTIONABLE verdict means all four gates pass — dip and rally both meaningful, the dip-then-rally sequence is likely enough to plan around, and the reward per unit risked is acceptable. Otherwise this horizon isn't a swing setup — either trend-follow at current price or stand aside.

Market mood — is the stock trending or choppy? downtrend · 47% confidence
What this is A statistical model classifies the past ~60 sessions into one of five regimes (uptrend-quiet, uptrend-noisy, downtrend, sideways, crisis). The current regime + its confidence drive (1) the daily drift parameter inside Monte Carlo (Layer 1 of conviction) and (2) the Layer-3 ENTER veto when state is downtrend/crisis with ≥70% confidence.
Current state downtrend — Sustained downward trend with persistent selling pressure — historically these don't reverse quickly. 47% confidence

IPGP is in a sustained downtrend (47% confidence). Observed features over last 60 sessions: trend -81.4%/yr implied, realized vol 72%/yr, vol-of-vol 4%. 17 sessions in this regime — recent flip. Nearest alternative is crisis at 22% — the call is at a boundary, treat with care.
What this means for you Drift fed to MC reflects regime direction. Watch for confidence ≥ 70% if state is downtrend/crisis — that threshold triggers the Layer-3 ENTER veto.
How wild are the daily moves? 72% realized · 30d forecast 72% (→ flat)
What this is Realized volatility = the standard deviation of daily returns, scaled to an annual figure (multiply by √252). A 30% annualized vol means the stock's typical year-over-year price swing is about 30% of its own price. GARCH(1,1) is a model that captures how today's vol depends on recent vol (vol tends to cluster — calm follows calm, wild follows wild).
Current realized vol (60d trailing)72.0% annualized≈ 4.5% typical daily move
Forecast end-of-30d72.4% annualized
Forecast end-of-60d72.0% annualized
Confidence band (set by tier)moderateTier A tight, B moderate, C wide
What this means for you Vol forecast roughly flat — set stop/target distances proportional to current realized vol. Higher vol also means the dip-entry and rally-sell zones in the price-levels panel will be further from current price — the model expects more movement in both directions, so the actionable levels spread out.
Is the price fair? $76.57 vs FV mean $135.50 (-2.58σ)
What this is Multi-method triangulation of fundamental value: forward P/E vs sector peers, discounted-cash-flow where input quality is sufficient, and recent comparable transactions. The output is a RANGE (not a point estimate) reflecting genuine uncertainty in valuation. The σ figure measures how far today's market price sits above (+) or below (−) the range mean, in units of the range's own standard deviation: ±1σ means "roughly within the normal valuation range"; ±2σ means "meaningfully outside it."
Fair-value range (low — mean — high)$44.11 — $135.50 — $135.50

Sanity check — do the two forecast methods agree? all 6 comparisons agree — no haircut
What this is We compute the same forecast (P(target hit first), P(stop hit first), expected value) two completely different ways: random simulation (50,000 Monte Carlo paths) AND solving a deterministic differential equation. The methods make different mathematical assumptions — when they agree, the answer is robust; when they diverge, at least one is being stretched by unusual conditions, so we don't know which to trust. Conviction gets a Layer-2 haircut when they disagree by more than 5.0pp.
Headline All 6 comparisons agree within tolerance — both methods independently arrive at the same answer. No Layer-2 haircut applied to conviction.

Is this stock behaving like its label? ⚠ watchlist B → measured C (stricter)
What this is Each ticker has a tier label you set in the watchlist — A mega-cap mature (think NVDA-like), B mid-cap growth (think AMAT-like), C small-cap fat-tailed (think IONQ-like). The pipeline uses your label to pick which statistical assumptions to run. This panel independently measures the stock's actual recent behavior — vol, vol-of-vol, liquidity, history — and tells you whether the label you set still matches reality. If the math has migrated tiers (e.g. a B-anchored name now behaving like C), the conviction confidence gets a Layer-2 haircut.

What this is A statistical model classifies the past ~60 sessions into one of five regimes (uptrend-quiet, uptrend-noisy, downtrend, sideways, crisis). The current regime + its confidence drive (1) the daily drift parameter inside Monte Carlo (Layer 1 of conviction) and (2) the Layer-3 ENTER veto when state is downtrend/crisis with ≥70% confidence.

How wild are the daily moves? 60% realized · 30d forecast 69% (↑ rising)
What this is Realized volatility = the standard deviation of daily returns, scaled to an annual figure (multiply by √252). A 30% annualized vol means the stock's typical year-over-year price swing is about 30% of its own price. GARCH(1,1) is a model that captures how today's vol depends on recent vol (vol tends to cluster — calm follows calm, wild follows wild).
Current realized vol (60d trailing)59.7% annualized≈ 3.8% typical daily move
Forecast end-of-30d69.5% annualized
Forecast end-of-60d73.4% annualized
Confidence band (set by tier)wideTier A tight, B moderate, C wide
What this means for you Vol forecast to rise — choppier days ahead. Stops and targets should be set wider to avoid being shaken out by normal noise. Higher vol also means the dip-entry and rally-sell zones in the price-levels panel will be further from current price — the model expects more movement in both directions, so the actionable levels spread out.
Is the price fair? $92.51 vs FV mean $945.29 (-4.34σ)
What this is Multi-method triangulation of fundamental value: forward P/E vs sector peers, discounted-cash-flow where input quality is sufficient, and recent comparable transactions. The output is a RANGE (not a point estimate) reflecting genuine uncertainty in valuation. The σ figure measures how far today's market price sits above (+) or below (−) the range mean, in units of the range's own standard deviation: ±1σ means "roughly within the normal valuation range"; ±2σ means "meaningfully outside it."
Fair-value range (low — mean — high)$160.00 — $945.29 — $945.29
Current market price$92.51-4.34σ vs FV mean
Methods contributingForward P/E peers, Analyst PT consensus
What this means for you Meaningfully cheap — well below the fair-value mean. Supports new entries (no Layer-3 veto from valuation).
Sanity check — do the two forecast methods agree? all 6 comparisons agree — no haircut
What this is We compute the same forecast (P(target hit first), P(stop hit first), expected value) two completely different ways: random simulation (50,000 Monte Carlo paths) AND solving a deterministic differential equation. The methods make different mathematical assumptions — when they agree, the answer is robust; when they diverge, at least one is being stretched by unusual conditions, so we don't know which to trust. Conviction gets a Layer-2 haircut when they disagree by more than 5.0pp.
Headline All 6 comparisons agree within tolerance — both methods independently arrive at the same answer. No Layer-2 haircut applied to conviction.

What this is Each ticker has a tier label you set in the watchlist — A mega-cap mature (think NVDA-like), B mid-cap growth (think AMAT-like), C small-cap fat-tailed (think IONQ-like). Swing trade — planned dip → rally Wait for the cheaper entry, sell at the realistic peak — vs the trend-follow path above (buy now, ride the +1σ target)
Derived from the same 50,000 Monte Carlo paths as the Price Levels above, but read for a different question: would patient buying at a dip and planned selling at a rally beat buying at current price? An ACTIONABLE verdict means all four gates pass — dip and rally both meaningful, the dip-then-rally sequence is likely enough to plan around, and the reward per unit risked is acceptable. Otherwise this horizon isn't a swing setup — either trend-follow at current price or stand aside.

Market mood — is the stock trending or choppy? uptrend_quiet · 44% confidence
What this is A statistical model classifies the past ~60 sessions into one of five regimes (uptrend-quiet, uptrend-noisy, downtrend, sideways, crisis). The current regime + its confidence drive (1) the daily drift parameter inside Monte Carlo (Layer 1 of conviction) and (2) the Layer-3 ENTER veto when state is downtrend/crisis with ≥70% confidence.
Current state uptrend_quiet — Sustained upward trend with normal day-to-day price swings — the calmest, most predictable bullish regime. 44% confidence
Days in this regime61 sessionslonger = more durable

How wild are the daily moves? 26% realized · 30d forecast 32% (↑ rising)
What this is Realized volatility = the standard deviation of daily returns, scaled to an annual figure (multiply by √252). A 30% annualized vol means the stock's typical year-over-year price swing is about 30% of its own price. GARCH(1,1) is a model that captures how today's vol depends on recent vol (vol tends to cluster — calm follows calm, wild follows wild).
Current realized vol (60d trailing)25.7% annualized≈ 1.6% typical daily move
Forecast end-of-30d31.5% annualized
Forecast end-of-60d33.6% annualized
Confidence band (set by tier)tightTier A tight, B moderate, C wide
What this means for you Vol forecast to rise — choppier days ahead. Stops and targets should be set wider to avoid being shaken out by normal noise. Higher vol also means the dip-entry and rally-sell zones in the price-levels panel will be further from current price — the model expects more movement in both directions, so the actionable levels spread out.
Is the price fair? $388.61 vs FV mean $289.50 (+1.43σ)
What this is Multi-method triangulation of fundamental value: forward P/E vs sector peers, discounted-cash-flow where input quality is sufficient, and recent comparable transactions. The output is a RANGE (not a point estimate) reflecting genuine uncertainty in valuation. The σ figure measures how far today's market price sits above (+) or below (−) the range mean, in units of the range's own standard deviation: ±1σ means "roughly within the normal valuation range"; ±2σ means "meaningfully outside it."
Fair-value range (low — mean — high)$214.26 — $289.50 — $491.00

Sanity check — do the two forecast methods agree? all 6 comparisons agree — no haircut
What this is We compute the same forecast (P(target hit first), P(stop hit first), expected value) two completely different ways: random simulation (50,000 Monte Carlo paths) AND solving a deterministic differential equation. The methods make different mathematical assumptions — when they agree, the answer is robust; when they diverge, at least one is being stretched by unusual conditions, so we don't know which to trust. Conviction gets a Layer-2 haircut when they disagree by more than 5.0pp.
Headline All 6 comparisons agree within tolerance — both methods independently arrive at the same answer. No Layer-2 haircut applied to conviction.

Is this stock behaving like its label? ✓ watchlist A → measured A (match)
What this is Each ticker has a tier label you set in the watchlist — A mega-cap mature (think NVDA-like), B mid-cap growth (think AMAT-like), C small-cap fat-tailed (think IONQ-like). The pipeline uses your label to pick which statistical assumptions to run. This panel independently measures the stock's actual recent behavior — vol, vol-of-vol, liquidity, history — and tells you whether the label you set still matches reality. If the math has migrated tiers (e.g. a B-anchored name now behaving like C), the conviction confidence gets a Layer-2 haircut.

Market mood — is the stock trending or choppy? uptrend_quiet · 48% confidence
What this is A statistical model classifies the past ~60 sessions into one of five regimes (uptrend-quiet, uptrend-noisy, downtrend, sideways, crisis). The current regime + its confidence drive (1) the daily drift parameter inside Monte Carlo (Layer 1 of conviction) and (2) the Layer-3 ENTER veto when state is downtrend/crisis with ≥70% confidence.

How wild are the daily moves? 18% realized · 30d forecast 19% (↑ rising)
What this is Realized volatility = the standard deviation of daily returns, scaled to an annual figure (multiply by √252). A 30% annualized vol means the stock's typical year-over-year price swing is about 30% of its own price. GARCH(1,1) is a model that captures how today's vol depends on recent vol (vol tends to cluster — calm follows calm, wild follows wild).
Current realized vol (60d trailing)18.0% annualized≈ 1.1% typical daily move

Forecast end-of-60d19.6% annualized

Higher vol also means the dip-entry and rally-sell zones in the price-levels panel will be further from current price — the model expects more movement in both directions, so the actionable levels spread out.
Is the price fair? $68.29 vs FV mean $71.12 (-3.98σ)
What this is Multi-method triangulation of fundamental value: forward P/E vs sector peers, discounted-cash-flow where input quality is sufficient, and recent comparable transactions. The output is a RANGE (not a point estimate) reflecting genuine uncertainty in valuation. The σ figure measures how far today's market price sits above (+) or below (−) the range mean, in units of the range's own standard deviation: ±1σ means "roughly within the normal valuation range"; ±2σ means "meaningfully outside it."
Fair-value range (low — mean — high)$71.00 — $71.12 — $71.12
Current market price$68.29-3.98σ vs FV mean
Methods contributingForward P/E peers, Analyst PT consensus
What this means for you Meaningfully cheap — well below the fair-value mean. Supports new entries (no Layer-3 veto from valuation).
Sanity check — do the two forecast methods agree? all 6 comparisons agree — no haircut
What this is We compute the same forecast (P(target hit first), P(stop hit first), expected value) two completely different ways: random simulation (50,000 Monte Carlo paths) AND solving a deterministic differential equation. The methods make different mathematical assumptions — when they agree, the answer is robust; when they diverge, at least one is being stretched by unusual conditions, so we don't know which to trust. Conviction gets a Layer-2 haircut when they disagree by more than 5.0pp.
Headline All 6 comparisons agree within tolerance — both methods independently arrive at the same answer. No Layer-2 haircut applied to conviction.

Is this stock behaving like its label? ✓ watchlist B → measured B (match)
What this is Each ticker has a tier label you set in the watchlist — A mega-cap mature (think NVDA-like), B mid-cap growth (think AMAT-like), C small-cap fat-tailed (think IONQ-like). The pipeline uses your label to pick which statistical assumptions to run. This panel independently measures the stock's actual recent behavior — vol, vol-of-vol, liquidity, history — and tells you whether the label you set still matches reality. If the math has migrated tiers (e.g. a B-anchored name now behaving like C), the conviction confidence gets a Layer-2 haircut.

Swing trade — planned dip → rally Wait for the cheaper entry, sell at the realistic peak — vs the trend-follow path above (buy now, ride the +1σ target)
Derived from the same 50,000 Monte Carlo paths as the Price Levels above, but read for a different question: would patient buying at a dip and planned selling at a rally beat buying at current price? An ACTIONABLE verdict means all four gates pass — dip and rally both meaningful, the dip-then-rally sequence is likely enough to plan around, and the reward per unit risked is acceptable. Otherwise this horizon isn't a swing setup — either trend-follow at current price or stand aside.

What's missing
  • Rally heightRally is 1.1% above current — needs 3.0%+. Would flip if price drops ~1.9% or if vol widens so the rally percentile extends further up to roughly $495.56.
  • YieldYield (dip→rally) is 4.5% — needs 15.0%+. This is a structural property of the stock's volatility profile: low-vol names rarely produce 15.0%+ swings in the horizon. Would flip only if implied vol rises (gap to close: 10.5 pp).
  • Sequence probabilityP(dip-then-rally) is 10% — needs 30%+. Gap is 20 percentage points. Under pure GBM dynamics, sequence prob is hard to push higher without a real catalyst (earnings bounce, mean-reversion regime, sector rotation). Refresh after the next material event.
  • Reward/RiskR:R is 0.00× — needs 2.0×+. Swing stop is undefined, so risk distance can't be computed reliably.
  • Swing stopNo safe stop level below dip — even the broadest cohort recovers < 75%. This means the path distribution is heavily skewed downward (paths fall and rarely come back). Stop is undefined; trade is too risky to plan around the dip-entry. Refresh after any material shift in drift or vol.
